T<sub>as</sub>k <sub>execu</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s</sub> f<sub>orma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>as a</sub> POMDP $\langle S , \mathcal { O } , \mathcal { A } , \mathcal { T } , \mathcal { R } \rangle$ At each step t the agent receives observation $o _ { t }$ (screenshot/execution feedback), takes action $a _ { t }$ <sub>, an</sub>d t<sub>rans</sub>iti<sub>ons</sub> t<sub>o</sub> $s _ { t + 1 }$ <sub>.</sub> P<sub>o</sub>l<sub>-</sub> icy π conditions on the full history $m _ { t } = \{ g , o _ { 0 } , a _ { 0 } , \ldots , o _ { t } \}$ <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $g$ i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d <sub>na</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l<sub>-</sub>l<sub>anguage</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> E<sub>p</sub>i<sub>so</sub>d<sub>es</sub> terminate on: DONE (a<sub>g</sub>ent declares success), FAIL (a<sub>g</sub>ent declares infeasible), or ste<sub>p</sub>-bud<sub>g</sub>et exhaustion (default 50 ste<sub>p</sub>s). A WAIT action is available for slow GUI res<sub>p</sub>onses.

Fi<sub>gure</sub> 3<sub>:</sub> O<sub>verv</sub>i<sub>ew</sub> <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> M<sub>at</sub>T<sub>ool</sub>B<sub>ench</sub> f<sub>ramewor</sub>k<sub>.</sub> T<sub>as</sub>k<sub>s</sub> <sub>are</sub> di<sub>spa</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>cen</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>l <sub>runner</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>one</sub> <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>ree</sub> <sub>spec</sub>i<sub>a</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d agents: the GUI Agent operates materials science desktop applications (e.g., JADE, VESTA, Avantage) via screenshot perception and coordinate-based interaction; the Code Agent generates Python scripts to query materials database APIs (e.g., MP, OQMD, OPTIMADE); and the Origin Agent combines GUI interaction with OriginPro script generation for experimental data plotting. GUI Actions. GUI tasks use a hierarchical Computer interface with five sub-modules: Mouse for coordinate-based clickin<sub>g</sub>, scrollin<sub>g</sub>, and dra<sub>gg</sub>in<sub>g</sub>, Keyboard for in<sub>p</sub>ut and hotke<sub>y</sub>s, Clipboard for co<sub>py</sub>-<sub>p</sub>aste, OS for <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>g</sub>ram launch, and WindowManager for fuzz<sub>y</sub>-match a<sub>pp</sub>lication switchi<sub>ng.</sub> A<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons are</sub> t<sub>ransm</sub>itt<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as</sub> P<sub>y</sub>th<sub>on co</sub>d<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ngs execu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d via exec() on the VM. The full API is in A<sub>pp</sub>endix C, T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 6<sub>.</sub>

C<sub>o</sub>d<sub>e</sub> <sub>a</sub>nd Ori<sub>g</sub>in A<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n<sub>s.</sub> C<sub>o</sub>d<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>s</sub> <sub>re</sub>d<sub>uce</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>e-</sub> <sub>an</sub>d<sub>-correc</sub>t l<sub>oop:</sub> th<sub>e agen</sub>t <sub>wr</sub>it<sub>es a comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e</sub> P<sub>y</sub>th<sub>on scr</sub>i<sub>p</sub>t <sub>per</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ep,</sub> <sub>execu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> <sub>an</sub> i<sub>so</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>v</sub>i<sub>r</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>env</sub>i<sub>ronmen</sub>t <sub>per</sub> domain; stdout/stderr/return-code are fed back for selfcorrection. Before d<sub>ep</sub>l<sub>oymen</sub>t i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> VM<sub>,</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>wor</sub>kfl<sub>ows were cross-c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>e</sub>d <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t <sub>ven</sub>d<sub>or</sub> t<sub>u</sub>t<sub>or</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l<sub>s, pu</sub>bli<sub>c opera</sub>ti<sub>on v</sub>id<sub>eos, an</sub>d d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>n</sub> d<sub>ocumen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Si<sub>m</sub>il<sub>ar</sub> l<sub>ow-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>ons may recur across</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>s,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>ar</sub>tif<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>s, so</sub>ft<sub>ware s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es, an</sub>d <sub>scor</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>cr</sub>it<sub>er</sub>i<sub>a</sub> dif<sub>er.</sub>

The Computer object provides a unified Python API for <sub>age</sub>nt<sub>–</sub>VM int<sub>e</sub>r<sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n<sub>.</sub> C<sub>o</sub>mm<sub>a</sub>nd<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub>r<sub>e</sub> <sub>se</sub>nt <sub>v</sub>i<sub>a</sub> HTTP POST <sub>an</sub>d <sub>execu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>server-s</sub>id<sub>e</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>a</sub> <sub>compac</sub>t <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>space</sub> <sub>cover</sub>i<sub>ng mouse,</sub> k<sub>ey</sub>b<sub>oar</sub>d<sub>, c</sub>li<sub>p</sub>b<sub>oar</sub>d<sub>, program con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>, an</sub>d <sub>w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow managemen</sub>t<sub>.</sub> T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 6 <sub>summar</sub>i<sub>zes</sub> th<sub>e comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>er</sub>f<sub>ace an</sub>d it<sub>s execu</sub>ti<sub>on cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s.</sub>

Table 6: Com<sub>p</sub>lete Computer action interface and execution constraints on the Windows VM. Calls are sent as P<sub>y</sub>thon strin<sub>g</sub>s via HTTP POST and executed server-side with exec(code, {"computer": computer}).
<table><tr><td>Module</td><td>Method</td><td>Parameters</td><td>Description</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="6"></td><td>move_id(id)</td><td>Element ID (int)</td><td>Optional; available only when SoM or accessibility-tree observations are enabled. Not used in the reported screenshot-only experiments</td></tr><tr><td>move_abs (x, y)</td><td>Normalized coords ∈ [0, 1]</td><td>Move cursor to a relative screen position; (0, 0)=top-left, (1, 1)=bottom-</td></tr><tr><td>single_click()</td><td></td><td>right Left single-click at the current cursor position</td></tr><tr><td>double_click()</td><td></td><td>Left double-click at the current cursor position</td></tr><tr><td>right_click()</td><td></td><td>Right-click at the current cursor position</td></tr><tr><td>scroll(dir)</td><td>&quot;up&quot;/ &quot;down&quot;</td><td>Scroll the active window by 400 units in the given direction</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="2"></td><td>drag(x, y)†</td><td>Screen pixel coords (int)</td><td>Left-button drag from current position to the target screen coordinates</td></tr><tr><td>write(text)†</td><td>ASCII string</td><td>Type text character-by-character via pyautogui. write (); suitable only for short labels and filenames</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="4"></td><td>press (key)</td><td>Key name or combo</td><td>Press a single key (e.g. &quot;f5&quot;, &quot;enter&quot;) or hotkey combo (e.g. &quot;ctrl+a&quot;,&quot;alt+4&quot;)</td></tr><tr><td>copy_text (text)</td><td>Unicode string (optional)</td><td>Copy text to the system clipboard via pyperclip. copy () ; supports full Unicode. If called with no argument, sends Ct rl +C to copy the</td></tr><tr><td>paste()</td><td></td><td>current selection Paste clipboard content at the current focus via Ctr1 +V</td></tr><tr><td>view_clipboard()</td><td></td><td>Return the current clipboard text as a Python string (via</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="4"></td><td>copy_image (id)</td><td>Element ID (int)</td><td>pyperclip.paste()) Crop the SoM element&#x27;s bounding box from the screenshot and write it</td></tr><tr><td>image-copy note</td><td>Element ID + description</td><td>to the clipboard as a DIB bitmap via win32clipboard Used only when the observation includes SoM element IDs; screenshot-</td></tr><tr><td></td><td></td><td>only agents cannot call ID-based image copy reliably</td></tr><tr><td>open_program(name)</td><td></td><td>Launch the named program via start, then maximize and bring to foreground; no-op if not installed Maximize the specified window, or the current foreground window if</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="4"></td><td>maximize_window()</td><td>Window handle (opt.)</td><td>omitted, via win32gui</td></tr><tr><td>is_installed(name) program policy</td><td>Executable name (str) Application alias</td><td>Return True if the program is found in the Windows registry or PATH Prefer explicit program launch or window switching over desktop-icon</td></tr><tr><td></td><td></td><td>clicking to reduce dependence on icon layout</td></tr><tr><td>switch_to _application(name)</td><td>App name (str)</td><td>Restore and maximize the closest-matching visible window (fuzzy match via difflib), then bring it to the foreground</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="4"></td><td>find_open _applications()</td><td></td><td>Return a list of title strings for all currently visible, non-empty windows</td></tr><tr><td>focus recovery</td><td>App name or title fragment</td><td>Used after setup, file dialogs, or script execution to recover the intended foreground application before the next action</td></tr><tr><td>transport</td><td>HTTP POST request</td><td>The client sends one Python code block per step to the VM-side executor;</td></tr><tr><td>namespace</td><td>computer object</td><td>the executor returns status, stdout, stderr, and any exception message The executed code receives only the exposed Computer object and</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="5"></td><td>coordinate frame</td><td>Normalized or pixel coords</td><td>standard Python runtime needed for action composition move_abs uses normalized screen coordinates, while drag uses raw</td></tr><tr><td></td><td></td><td>screen pixels; mixing the two is a common failure mode</td></tr><tr><td>Unicode policy</td><td>Clipboard preferred</td><td>Long scripts, non-ASCII text, and multi-line commands should be copied through the clipboard rather than typed through keyboard. write</td></tr><tr><td>error handling</td><td>JSON status payload</td><td>Invalid method calls, bad parameters, or backend exceptions return an error status; the episode continues unless the agent terminates</td></tr><tr><td>termination</td><td>Agent-level signal</td><td>The agent marks completion with the framework-level DONE decision;</td></tr></table>

## A<sub>ppe</sub>ndi<sub>x</sub> D<sub>.</sub> E<sub>xecu</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n En<sub>v</sub>ir<sub>o</sub>nm<sub>e</sub>nt <sub>a</sub>nd R<sub>ep</sub>r<sub>o</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ibilit<sub>y</sub>

F<sub>orma</sub>ll<sub>y,</sub> <sub>a</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k $x$ i<sub>s</sub> <sub>spec</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> <sub>an</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> $^ { g , }$ <sub>an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>-</sub> ti<sub>a</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>program</sub> $c ,$ and n scoring sub-criteria $\mathcal { C } ( x ) =$ $\{ ( g _ { i } , m _ { i } , w _ { i } ) \} _ { i = 1 } ^ { n }$ <sub>,</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $g _ { i }$ <sup>i</sup>s a <sub>g</sub>etter, $m _ { i }$ i<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> f<sub>unc-</sub> ti<sub>on,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d $w _ { i }$ is the criterion weight. After an agent trajectory terminates in final VM state v<sub>T</sub>,

$$
r _ { i } = m _ { i } ( g _ { i } ( v _ { T } ) , y _ { i } ) , \qquad r _ { i } \in [ 0 , 1 ] ,\tag{1}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $y _ { i }$ i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e re</sub>f<sub>erence va</sub>l<sub>ue or pre</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>e.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>score</sub> i<sub>s</sub>

$$
\operatorname { S c o r e } ( x ) = { \frac { \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { n } w _ { i } r _ { i } } { \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { n } w _ { i } } } , \qquad \operatorname { S R } ( x ) = \mathbb { I } [ \forall i , r _ { i } = 1 ] .\tag{2}

Origin tasks require a <sub>secon</sub>d <sub>re</sub>li<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> <sub>ana</sub>l<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>s</sub> b<sub>ecause</sub> fi<sub>gure</sub> <sub>qua</sub>lit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>no</sub>t f<sub>u</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>cap</sub>t<sub>ure</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> fil<sub>e</sub> <sub>ex</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ence.</sub> T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 14 th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> <sub>repor</sub>t<sub>s</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>pars</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>agreemen</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ith h<sub>uman</sub> <sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>ngs</sub> f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> aesthetic judge. Let E be the set of evaluated e<sub>p</sub>isodes in a domain, $T _ { e }$ th<sub>e</sub> <sub>num</sub>b<sub>er o</sub>f <sub>execu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>eps</sub> i<sub>n ep</sub>i<sub>so</sub>d<sub>e</sub> $e , s _ { e }$ th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>success</sub> i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>or, an</sub>d $d _ { e }$ <sub>an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>or</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e ep</sub>i<sub>so</sub>d<sub>e en</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>an</sub> a<sub>g</sub>ent-issued DONE. We com<sub>p</sub>ute

$$
{ \mathrm { S t e p s } } = { \frac { 1 } { | \mathcal { E } | } } \sum _ { e \in \mathcal { E } } T _ { e } , \qquad { \mathrm { S t e p s } } ^ { * } = { \frac { \sum _ { e \in \mathcal { E } } s _ { e } T _ { e } } { \sum _ { e \in \mathcal { E } } s _ { e } } } ,\tag{3}
$$

with Steps<sup>∗</sup> left undefined when no episode succeeds. False t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s measure</sub>d <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y among se</sub>lf<sub>-</sub>t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>runs:</sub>

$$
\mathrm { F T R } = \frac { \sum _ { e \in { \mathcal { E } } } d _ { e } \mathbb { I } [ s _ { e } = 0 ] } { \sum _ { e \in { \mathcal { E } } } d _ { e } } .\tag{4}
$$

## GUI Agent: System Prompt (condensed)

You are MatGUI Helper, an AI agent that controls materials-science GUI software on a user’s computer to complete analytical tasks. You interact with desktop applications such as Jade (XRD), Avantage (XPS), VESTA (crystal structure), DigitalMicrograph, and Materials Studio.

Guidelines: plan efficiently; execute ONE action per step; verify progress using the previous screenshot; use coordinates and hotkeys carefully.

Outputs per step: screen analysis → multi-step plan → next-step rationale → decision block (COMMAND / DONE / FAIL / WAIT) → python code block → memory update.

## Per-application startup hints (hint mode only):

• JADE (ablation-critical): The “Read Pattern Files” database dialog is PRE-OPENED at startup. Close it first — it accepts only reference patterns, not sample data files. Attempting to load task files here causes an unrecoverable deadlock.

• Avantage: A file Open dialog is PRE-TRIGGERED (Ctrl+O). Type the file path and press Enter directly. Workflow is self-evident from the interface; not included in the ablation.

• VESTA: A file Open dialog is PRE-TRIGGERED. Type the path and press Enter. Workflow is self-evident from the interface; not included in the ablation.

• DM (ablation-critical): A file Open dialog is PRE-TRIGGERED. At startup a floating panel (“Histogram”, “Images”, “Status”, etc.) occupies the left side of the workspace — close it first. Without this hint, the panel can obscure the filename shown in the title bar, directly disrupting the evaluator’s file-import check and causing incorrect scoring.

• Materials Studio: Maximize the window before any other action.

• Origin: The target .opju file is already open and Code Builder is already open (Alt+4 pre-pressed).

## Code Agent: System Prompt (condensed)

You are MatCode Helper, an AI agent that writes Python scripts to query materials-science databases and APIs.

Output: a single, complete, self-contained python code block that (1) queries the specified database, (2) processes results, and (3) writes output to the exact file path given in the task.

Rules: Python 3.10; mp-api 0.39.5 / emmet-core 0.78.7;   
no GUI windows; UTF-8 output; handle errors gracefully;   
do not include venv activation.

## One-shot API examples (hint mode only):

• MP: use the Materials Project summary-search API with task-specific elements and returned fields.

• OQMD: use requests.get(...) on the formationenergy endpoint with retry logic.

• OPTIMADE: requests.get( "https://optimade.materialsproject.org/v1/structures", params={"filter": "...", "page\_limit": 100}) with auto-pagination

• Pymatgen: Structure.from\_file(path),   
SpacegroupAnalyzer, Poscar

## Origin Agent: System Prompt (condensed)

You are an AI agent that controls OriginLab to complete plotting and data-analysis tasks by writing Python (originpro) scripts in Code Builder.

## Standard workflow (hint mode):

1. Data file is already open in Origin’s worksheet — do NOT reopen it.

2. Code Builder is already open (Alt+4 pre-pressed) — go straight to step 3.

3. Press Ctrl+N to create a new Python file; name it to match the plot type (raman.py, xrd.py, xps.py, ftir.py, . . . ).

4. Paste the full script via clipboard: copy\_text("...script...") → paste(). Never use keyboard.write() for scripts ASCII-only, corrupts Unicode.

5. Press F5 to run. Fix errors by clearing (Ctrl+A, Delete) and re-pasting.

6. Verify the output file exists at the specified path, then mark DONE.

Baseline mode (no\_hint): No workflow instructions are provided. The agent must independently discover whether to use Code Builder or GUI-only interaction, and must write the entire plotting script without a template. Code Builder is not pre-opened, removing the implicit workflow signal.

Available packages: originpro, numpy, pandas, matplotlib, scipy, re, os, math.
